At work, I can sit staring at the same audit workpaper for, like, twenty minutes before I even get my hand to move and type something. People have started noticing I’m slower, and I thought maybe I should come in before it gets worse... if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up with this really heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. Like the day is already… lost, before I’ve even gotten out of bed. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a lack of energy or feeling fatigued almost every day, even when you haven’t done much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes. Even if I’ve been in bed all night, I wake up feeling like I’ve been up for days, like during a really bad audit close. And then small things—showering, or trying to sort through insurance paperwork—can kind of wipe me out. I know that sounds maybe a little ridiculous, but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating much less or more than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m not trying to diet or anything. Food just hasn’t been very appealing, so I kind of make myself eat because I know I’m supposed to. And I’ve noticed my work skirts are looser than they used to be, which… I guess means I’ve lost some weight. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t interest you anymore or feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I used to be pretty good about planning hikes, taking photos, cooking on Sundays… I even volunteered at the library. And now it’s not that I hate those things, exactly. They just feel like tasks on a checklist, and I can’t seem to care enough to start them. I don’t know if that counts, but… it feels different than just being busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or worthless than usual, even about small things?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, I feel bad about things sometimes, like not getting enough done, but I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly. Maybe I’m being too hard on myself, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down, or maybe you feel more restless and can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. I’ve tried to tell myself it’s just being tired or distracted, but it’s been noticeable. At work I’ll sit there staring at the same audit workpaper for, I don’t know, twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type anything. \n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but people have started noticing I’m not keeping up like I normally do, so… I guess yes, that’s been happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People have started noticing I’m slower, and I thought maybe I should come in before it gets worse... if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up with this really heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. Like the day is already… lost, before I’ve even gotten out of bed. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a lack of energy or feeling fatigued almost every day, even when you haven’t done much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes. Even if I’ve been in bed all night, I wake up feeling like I’ve been up for days, like during a really bad audit close. And then small things—showering, or trying to sort through insurance paperwork—can kind of wipe me out. I know that sounds maybe a little ridiculous, but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating much less or more than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m not trying to diet or anything. Food just hasn’t been very appealing, so I kind of make myself eat because I know I’m supposed to. And I’ve noticed my work skirts are looser than they used to be, which… I guess means I’ve lost some weight. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t interest you anymore or feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I used to be pretty good about planning hikes, taking photos, cooking on Sundays… I even volunteered at the library. And now it’s not that I hate those things, exactly. They just feel like tasks on a checklist, and I can’t seem to care enough to start them. I don’t know if that counts, but… it feels different than just being busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or worthless than usual, even about small things?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, I feel bad about things sometimes, like not getting enough done, but I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly. Maybe I’m being too hard on myself, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down, or maybe you feel more restless and can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. I’ve tried to tell myself it’s just being tired or distracted, but it’s been noticeable. At work I’ll sit there staring at the same audit workpaper for, I don’t know, twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type anything. \n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but people have started noticing I’m not keeping up like I normally do, so… I guess yes, that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when nothing — not even things you usually care about — felt enjoyable or worth doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound like I never care about anything, but the things I used to do without thinking—planning hikes, taking photos, cooking on Sundays, volunteering at the library—they mostly just feel like… obligations now. Like items on a checklist. And I keep meaning to do them, but I can’t really get myself to care enough to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, where you felt down and lost interest in things, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I’ve had times before where I was stressed or kind of low, you know, especially when work was heavy, but… I don’t know if it was like this.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say “episodes” if that means something more specific. This feels more noticeable than just a rough patch, I guess, but I’m not sure if I’m remembering past times accurately. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way for several weeks or more, where it really affected your mood, energy, or ability to enjoy things?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not clearly, anyway. I’ve had stressful stretches where I wasn’t exactly myself, but I could usually still function and enjoy things once work calmed down or I got some rest.\n\nThis feels… different enough that I’m having trouble comparing it to anything before. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not the most helpful answer. I just don’t want to overstate it if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time before when you felt this level of low mood and loss of interest, where it lasted for weeks and really affected your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… at least not that I can clearly point to. I’ve had rough periods before, but I don’t remember anything that lasted for weeks and really disrupted everything like this.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate, not just give the “right” answer. I guess this feels like the first time it’s been this hard to shake or work around.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt down most of the day, nearly every day, and lost interest in most things — even if it didn’t disrupt your life as much as this current time?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just sort of fitting normal stress into that description.\n\nI’ve had times where I was down or less interested in things, but whether it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks… I can’t really say that clearly. Nothing comes to mind that I’d feel confident calling that. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you think of a time before this current period when you had a similar low mood and loss of interest in things that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it wasn’t quite as severe as now?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I keep circling on this… but no, I can’t really think of a time before now that clearly fits that. I’ve had stressful or sad periods, but not where I remember losing interest in things for at least two weeks in that same way.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it just to make an answer fit. From what I can recall, this is the first time it’s felt like this.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I know that sounds maybe a little ridiculous, but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating much less or more than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m not trying to diet or anything. Food just hasn’t been very appealing, so I kind of make myself eat because I know I’m supposed to. And I’ve noticed my work skirts are looser than they used to be, which… I guess means I’ve lost some weight. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t interest you anymore or feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I used to be pretty good about planning hikes, taking photos, cooking on Sundays… I even volunteered at the library. And now it’s not that I hate those things, exactly. They just feel like tasks on a checklist, and I can’t seem to care enough to start them. I don’t know if that counts, but… it feels different than just being busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or worthless than usual, even about small things?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, I feel bad about things sometimes, like not getting enough done, but I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly. Maybe I’m being too hard on myself, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down, or maybe you feel more restless and can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. I’ve tried to tell myself it’s just being tired or distracted, but it’s been noticeable. At work I’ll sit there staring at the same audit workpaper for, I don’t know, twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type anything. \n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but people have started noticing I’m not keeping up like I normally do, so… I guess yes, that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when nothing — not even things you usually care about — felt enjoyable or worth doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound like I never care about anything, but the things I used to do without thinking—planning hikes, taking photos, cooking on Sundays, volunteering at the library—they mostly just feel like… obligations now. Like items on a checklist. And I keep meaning to do them, but I can’t really get myself to care enough to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, where you felt down and lost interest in things, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I’ve had times before where I was stressed or kind of low, you know, especially when work was heavy, but… I don’t know if it was like this.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say “episodes” if that means something more specific. This feels more noticeable than just a rough patch, I guess, but I’m not sure if I’m remembering past times accurately. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way for several weeks or more, where it really affected your mood, energy, or ability to enjoy things?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not clearly, anyway. I’ve had stressful stretches where I wasn’t exactly myself, but I could usually still function and enjoy things once work calmed down or I got some rest.\n\nThis feels… different enough that I’m having trouble comparing it to anything before. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not the most helpful answer. I just don’t want to overstate it if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time before when you felt this level of low mood and loss of interest, where it lasted for weeks and really affected your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… at least not that I can clearly point to. I’ve had rough periods before, but I don’t remember anything that lasted for weeks and really disrupted everything like this.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate, not just give the “right” answer. I guess this feels like the first time it’s been this hard to shake or work around.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt down most of the day, nearly every day, and lost interest in most things — even if it didn’t disrupt your life as much as this current time?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just sort of fitting normal stress into that description.\n\nI’ve had times where I was down or less interested in things, but whether it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks… I can’t really say that clearly. Nothing comes to mind that I’d feel confident calling that. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you think of a time before this current period when you had a similar low mood and loss of interest in things that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it wasn’t quite as severe as now?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I keep circling on this… but no, I can’t really think of a time before now that clearly fits that. From what I can recall, this is the first time it’s felt like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time before this current period when you felt down most of the day, nearly every day, and lost interest in most things for at least two weeks, to the point where it clearly affected your daily life — even if it wasn’t as severe as now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a lack of energy or feeling fatigued almost every day, even when you haven’t done much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes. Even if I’ve been in bed all night, I wake up feeling like I’ve been up for days, like during a really bad audit close. And then small things—showering, or trying to sort through insurance paperwork—can kind of wipe me out. I know that sounds maybe a little ridiculous, but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating much less or more than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m not trying to diet or anything. Food just hasn’t been very appealing, so I kind of make myself eat because I know I’m supposed to. And I’ve noticed my work skirts are looser than they used to be, which… I guess means I’ve lost some weight. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t interest you anymore or feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I used to be pretty good about planning hikes, taking photos, cooking on Sundays… I even volunteered at the library. And now it’s not that I hate those things, exactly. They just feel like tasks on a checklist, and I can’t seem to care enough to start them. I don’t know if that counts, but… it feels different than just being busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or worthless than usual, even about small things?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, I feel bad about things sometimes, like not getting enough done, but I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly. Maybe I’m being too hard on myself, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down, or maybe you feel more restless and can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more slowed down, I think. I’ve tried to tell myself it’s just being tired or distracted, but it’s been noticeable. At work I’ll sit there staring at the same audit workpaper for, I don’t know, twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type anything. \n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but people have started noticing I’m not keeping up like I normally do, so… I guess yes, that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when nothing — not even things you usually care about — felt enjoyable or worth doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound like I never care about anything, but the things I used to do without thinking—planning hikes, taking photos, cooking on Sundays, volunteering at the library—they mostly just feel like… obligations now. Like items on a checklist. And I keep meaning to do them, but I can’t really get myself to care enough to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, where you felt down and lost interest in things, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I’ve had times before where I was stressed or kind of low, you know, especially when work was heavy, but… I don’t know if it was like this.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say “episodes” if that means something more specific. This feels more noticeable than just a rough patch, I guess, but I’m not sure if I’m remembering past times accurately. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt this way for several weeks or more, where it really affected your mood, energy, or ability to enjoy things?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not clearly, anyway. I’ve had stressful stretches where I wasn’t exactly myself, but I could usually still function and enjoy things once work calmed down or I got some rest.\n\nThis feels… different enough that I’m having trouble comparing it to anything before. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not the most helpful answer. I just don’t want to overstate it if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time before when you felt this level of low mood and loss of interest, where it lasted for weeks and really affected your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… at least not that I can clearly point to. I’ve had rough periods before, but I don’t remember anything that lasted for weeks and really disrupted everything like this.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate, not just give the “right” answer. I guess this feels like the first time it’s been this hard to shake or work around.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt down most of the day, nearly every day, and lost interest in most things — even if it didn’t disrupt your life as much as this current time?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just sort of fitting normal stress into that description.\n\nI’ve had times where I was down or less interested in things, but whether it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks… I can’t really say that clearly. Nothing comes to mind that I’d feel confident calling that. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you think of a time before this current period when you had a similar low mood and loss of interest in things that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it wasn’t quite as severe as now?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I keep circling on this… but no, I can’t really think of a time before now that clearly fits that. I’ve had stressful or sad periods, but not where I remember losing interest in things for at least two weeks in that same way.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it just to make an answer fit. From what I can recall, this is the first time it’s felt like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time before this current period when you felt down most of the day, nearly every day, and lost interest in most things for at least two weeks, to the point where it clearly affected your daily life — even if it wasn’t as severe as now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. So I’d say no, not that I can identify.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was ever a time before this current period when you experienced a low mood and loss of interest in things that lasted for at least two weeks and clearly affected your daily life — even if it wasn’t as severe as it is now?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’ve kind of answered this already… but no, I really can’t recall a time before this that clearly fits that.\n\nThere were normal stressful stretches, or times I was unhappy, but not something I can point to where it was low mood plus losing interest for two weeks and it clearly affected my day-to-day life. At least, not that I remember. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I think… no, not really. I know I’ve said that a few times, I’m sorry.
